- [ ] **Step 3: Write the record** `docs/design/m6-loom-ui-native.md`:

```markdown
# M6: the web UI is loom-ui native (spec §7, spec §9 item 35)

Checked 2026-10-21 against loom-ui `4f535168` and Loom `c81d521a` with throwaway probes (not merged).

## 1. Where experiences are composed

| Question | Answer (what the probe or the source showed) |
|---|---|
| Can the browser compose and compile? | No. `compile` lives on `@loom-ui/core`'s main entry, which imports `node:crypto` (`src/alias.ts`); `seal` is on `/log` (Node only); `genesis`, `apply` and `defineSemanticCatalog` carry zod. `/browser` is the zod-free renderer slice: formats, `checkKindValue`, the action freshness helpers, container roles, no compile. |
| Can the server compose, seal, compile and answer handle loads? | Yes. `genesis` + a sealed experience + `compile(..., { surface: "web" })` gave no findings; `handleInventory(exp, { revisionOf })` listed handle, priority, overridable param names and a keyed revision; `serveHandles` with a registered data-provider kind (`docmind`), a Named reshaper and a request-scoped invoker answered `{state:"ready", value}`, with the principal threaded to the invoker. |
| Does the browser half work? | Yes, under happy-dom: `SplitHydrationController` + `HydrationStore` + `renderBlueprintDom` with `createChatLitRegistry()` and `referenceContainers()` drew tabs, `Table`, `Note`, `StatusCard`, `A2UIView`, `Chat`, `TextInput`, `SelectInput`; actions arrived stamped and `actionFreshness` said `fresh`. |

**Decision.** Compose, seal and compile in the shared, surface-neutral `@docmind/experiences` package, run by the DocMind server, once per view and surface, content-free; the catalogue resolves every entry on the `web` and `terminal` surfaces and the action vocabulary (`intentOf`) is shared. Serve the blueprint and inventory per view; hydrate through `POST /api/ui/hydrate` into the browser's `SplitHydrationController`. The browser imports only `@loom-ui/hydration/client`, `@loom-ui/core/browser` and `@loom-ui/renderer-lit`. The session's identity is closed over per request on the server; no handle parameter names a tenant or a user.

## 2. Kinds used

| View | Kinds |
|---|---|
| library | `Kpi` x2, `StatusCard`, `SelectInput`, `Table` (row actions, loom-ui first) |
| library-empty | `Note` |
| detail | `tabs` container (`referenceContainers()`): Summary `A2UIView`, Analysis `Kpi` x3 + `Table`, Content `A2UIView`; `StatusCard` above |
| sessions | `TextInput`, `Table` (row actions) |
| chat | `Chat` (reference markers and markdown, loom-ui first), `SelectInput`, `TextInput` |
| sources | `A2UIView` |

## 3. Gaps in loom-ui, and the verdicts

| Gap | Generic? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `Table` rows cannot emit an action (`lu-table.ts` has no `onAction`) | Yes: any admin table needs "open this row" | loom-ui first (Task 176a) |
| `Chat` draws `[[id]]` as literal text; no inline marker | Yes: any retrieval or footnoted transcript | loom-ui first (Task 176b) |
| Markdown in chat text | Yes: every assistant transcript; loom-ui refuses it today by design | loom-ui first, as an opt-in closed subset (Tasks 176c to 176k, open question 4) |
| `MediaInput` is one file with no drop target or progress | Generic but not needed | app-specific `dm-upload-zone` |
| `FormInput` has no `password`/`email` field | Generic follow-up | app-specific `dm-auth-form` for M6 |
| `Table` paging, selection, grouping | Generic follow-ups | not built (open questions 13, 15) |

## 4. The generic requests

The text of the loom-ui pull requests is the "PR body" of Tasks 176a to 176k of the M6 plan. They name no DocMind concept.
```

### Task 176a (loom-ui repo): `Table` row actions

**Repo and branch:** `~/workspace/loom-ui`, branch `feat/table-row-actions`, a draft PR, rebase-merged. Read its `AGENTS.md` first (Loom is consumed built; edit, rebuild, then test; `typecheck:tests` is a gate; every new `refuse`/message key has a test or catalogue entry).

**Why (generic).** A `Table` draws records but a user cannot act on one: no "open", "retry", "delete" on a row. Every list-and-act screen (invoices, tickets, devices) needs it. The host decides what an action means; the widget only reports which row and which action, stamped with the viewed version like every other widget action.

**Contract.** `TableValue` gains two optional fields, parallel to `rows`:
- `rowKeys?: string[]`: one stable key per row (`^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}$`, unique). It is what an action names; a row's position is not stable across a sort or a refresh.
- `rowActions?: TableRowAction[][]`: one list per row, at most 4 each; `TableRowAction = { id: /^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$/; label: 1..40 chars; primary?: boolean; destructive?: boolean }`; ids unique within a row; at most one `primary` per row; `rowActions` requires `rowKeys`.
- The widget draws a trailing actions cell with one button per action (name: `"<label>: <first cell>"`, so every button has a distinct accessible name), makes the first cell a button for the `primary` action, and emits `{ type: "TABLE_ROW_ACTION", widgetId, payload: { action, rowKey } }` through `onAction` (stamped by the renderer; the host's answer is handled like `dispatch` does for chat: no handler means the buttons are disabled). A destructive action is drawn with the destructive style and is never the primary one.
- A value that breaks a rule is refused by `checkKindValue` (the widget shows its visible error), like any other kind.
- Untouched: `columns`, `rows`, sorting (the keys and actions follow their row), windowing, every existing value.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/core/src/tableRows.ts`, `packages/core/src/tableRows.test.ts`, `packages/renderer-lit/src/widgets/tableRowActions.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/core/src/values.ts`, `packages/core/src/kindValues.ts`, `packages/core/src/browser.ts`, `packages/core/src/index.ts`, `packages/core/src/messages.ts`, `packages/renderer-lit/src/widgets/lu-table.ts`, `packages/core/README.md`, `docs/reference/composition.md`, the differential fixtures of `kindValues` (find them with `grep -rln "differential" packages/core/src`)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (core, zod-free, `tableRows.ts`): `TABLE_ROW_ACTION = "TABLE_ROW_ACTION"`; `MAX_ROW_ACTIONS = 4`; `interface TableRowAction`; `tableRowProblems(v: { rows: readonly unknown[]; rowKeys?: unknown; rowActions?: unknown }): { path: (string | number)[]; message: string }[]` (total; messages fixed and value-free). Exported from `@loom-ui/core` and `@loom-ui/core/browser`. `TableValue` gains `rowKeys?`, `rowActions?`.
- Produces (renderer): `lu-table` as above; message keys `lu.table.actions` ("Actions") and `lu.table.rowAction` ("{label}: {row}").

`packages/core/src/tableRows.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { checkKindValue } from "./browser";
import { TABLE_ROW_ACTION, tableRowProblems } from "./tableRows";
import { TableValue } from "./values";

const base = { columns: ["Name"], rows: [["a"], ["b"]] };
const open = { id: "open", label: "Open", primary: true };
const del = { id: "delete", label: "Delete", destructive: true };

describe("table row actions: the contract", () => {
  it("names the action type", () => expect(TABLE_ROW_ACTION).toBe("TABLE_ROW_ACTION"));

  it("accepts a table without keys or actions, with keys alone, and with keys and actions", () => {
    for (const v of [base, { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"] }, { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[open, del], []] }]) {
      expect(TableValue.safeParse(v).success).toBe(true);
      expect(checkKindValue("Table", v).ok).toBe(true);
      expect(tableRowProblems(v)).toEqual([]);
    }
  });

  it.each([
    ["a key per row", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1"] }, "rowKeys must have one key per row"],
    ["unique keys", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r1"] }, "rowKeys must be unique"],
    ["a plain key", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "bad key"] }, "rowKeys must be short identifiers"],
    ["keys for actions", { ...base, rowActions: [[], []] }, "rowActions need rowKeys"],
    ["a list per row", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[]] }, "rowActions must have one list per row"],
    ["at most four", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[open, del, { id: "a", label: "A" }, { id: "b", label: "B" }, { id: "c", label: "C" }], []] }, "a row has at most 4 actions"],
    ["one primary", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[open, { id: "edit", label: "Edit", primary: true }], []] }, "a row has at most one primary action"],
    ["unique ids", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[open, { id: "open", label: "Again" }], []] }, "action ids must be unique within a row"],
    ["a plain id", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[{ id: "Open!", label: "Open" }], []] }, "an action id must be lower-case words"],
    ["a short label", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[{ id: "open", label: "" }], []] }, "an action label is 1 to 40 characters"],
    ["no destructive primary", { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r2"], rowActions: [[{ id: "delete", label: "Delete", primary: true, destructive: true }], []] }, "a destructive action cannot be primary"],
  ])("refuses %s", (_name, v, message) => {
    expect(tableRowProblems(v).map((p) => p.message)).toContain(message);
    expect(TableValue.safeParse(v).success).toBe(false);
    const checked = checkKindValue("Table", v);
    expect(checked.ok).toBe(false);
  });

  it("the zod contract and the zod-free checker say the same thing (same messages, same paths)", () => {
    const bad = { ...base, rowKeys: ["r1", "r1"], rowActions: [[open, open], []] };
    const zod = TableValue.safeParse(bad);
    const messages = zod.success ? [] : zod.error.issues.map((i) => i.message);
    expect(messages).toEqual(tableRowProblems(bad).map((p) => p.message));
  });
});
```

`packages/renderer-lit/src/widgets/tableRowActions.test.ts` (use the harness the other widget tests use: read `widgets.test.ts`/`frameworkKinds.test.ts` for how they mount an element with `widget`, `value`, `onAction`):

```ts
import { TABLE_ROW_ACTION } from "@loom-ui/core/browser";
import { customWidget$ } from "@loom/blueprint-runtime";
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import "../elements/lu-table";

const b = { projectionId: "hydration", path: "values.h" };
beforeEach(() => vi.stubGlobal("IntersectionObserver", undefined));

type El = HTMLElement & { updateComplete: Promise<unknown> };
async function mount(value: unknown, onAction?: (a: unknown) => unknown): Promise<El> {
  const el = document.createElement("lu-table") as El;
  Object.assign(el, { widget: customWidget$({ kind: "Table", id: "t", title: "Records", binding: b, props: {} }), value, onAction });
  document.body.appendChild(el);
  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) { await Promise.resolve(); await el.updateComplete; }
  return el;
}
const q = <T extends Element>(el: El, sel: string) => el.shadowRoot?.querySelector(sel) as T;
const all = <T extends Element>(el: El, sel: string) => [...(el.shadowRoot?.querySelectorAll(sel) ?? [])] as T[];

const value = {
  columns: ["Name", "Size"],
  rows: [["bravo", 2], ["alpha", 1]],
  rowKeys: ["k-bravo", "k-alpha"],
  rowActions: [[{ id: "open", label: "Open", primary: true }, { id: "delete", label: "Delete", destructive: true }], [{ id: "open", label: "Open", primary: true }]],
};

describe("lu-table row actions", () => {
  it("draws one named button per action and a primary button in the first cell", async () => {
    const el = await mount(value, () => undefined);
    expect(all<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "td.row-actions button").map((x) => x.getAttribute("aria-label"))).toEqual(["Open: bravo", "Delete: bravo", "Open: alpha"]);
    expect(q<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "tbody tr:first-child td:first-child button")?.textContent?.trim()).toBe("bravo");
    expect(q(el, "th .sr-only")?.textContent).toBe("Actions");
  });

  it("reports the row's key and the action, and the key follows its row through a sort", async () => {
    const seen: unknown[] = [];
    const el = await mount(value, (a) => { seen.push(a); });
    q<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "button[aria-label='Delete: bravo']").click();
    q<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "thead th button").click(); // sort by Name, descending first
    q<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "thead th button").click(); // ascending: alpha first
    await el.updateComplete;
    q<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "tbody tr:first-child button[data-row-action='open']").click();
    expect(seen).toEqual([
      { type: TABLE_ROW_ACTION, widgetId: "t", payload: { action: "delete", rowKey: "k-bravo" } },
      { type: TABLE_ROW_ACTION, widgetId: "t", payload: { action: "open", rowKey: "k-alpha" } },
    ]);
  });

  it("disables the buttons when the host gave no handler, and draws nothing extra for a plain table", async () => {
    const el = await mount(value);
    expect(all<HTMLButtonElement>(el, "button[data-row-action]").every((x) => x.disabled)).toBe(true);
    const plain = await mount({ columns: ["Name"], rows: [["a"]] });
    expect(all(plain, "button[data-row-action]")).toEqual([]);
    expect(q(plain, "th.actions")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("shows the visible error for a value that breaks the contract (actions without keys)", async () => {
    const el = await mount({ columns: ["Name"], rows: [["a"]], rowActions: [[{ id: "open", label: "Open" }]] }, () => undefined);
    expect(q(el, "[data-state='error']")).not.toBeNull();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the core half.**

`packages/core/src/tableRows.ts`:

```ts
// ── Table row actions (U-table) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// A `Table` row can carry actions the host answers. Zod-free and DOM-free: the zod contract
// (`values.ts`) and the browser checker (`kindValues.ts`) both call `tableRowProblems`, so the
// two cannot disagree on a rule or on its words.

/** The action type a table row's button emits: `payload: { action, rowKey }`. */
export const TABLE_ROW_ACTION = "TABLE_ROW_ACTION";
export const MAX_ROW_ACTIONS = 4;

const ROW_KEY = /^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}$/;
const ACTION_ID = /^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$/;

export interface TableRowAction {
  readonly id: string;
  readonly label: string;
  readonly primary?: boolean;
  readonly destructive?: boolean;
}

export interface RowProblem {
  readonly path: (string | number)[];
  readonly message: string;
}

const isRecord = (v: unknown): v is Record<string, unknown> => typeof v === "object" && v !== null && !Array.isArray(v);

/** Why `rowKeys` / `rowActions` do not fit `rows`; empty when they do. Total on any input; messages never echo a value. */
export function tableRowProblems(v: { rows: readonly unknown[]; rowKeys?: unknown; rowActions?: unknown }): RowProblem[] {
  const out: RowProblem[] = [];
  const { rows, rowKeys, rowActions } = v;
  if (rowKeys !== undefined && Array.isArray(rowKeys)) {
    if (rowKeys.length !== rows.length) out.push({ path: ["rowKeys"], message: "rowKeys must have one key per row" });
    if (rowKeys.some((k) => typeof k !== "string" || !ROW_KEY.test(k))) out.push({ path: ["rowKeys"], message: "rowKeys must be short identifiers" });
    if (new Set(rowKeys).size !== rowKeys.length) out.push({ path: ["rowKeys"], message: "rowKeys must be unique" });
  }
  if (rowActions !== undefined && Array.isArray(rowActions)) {
    if (rowKeys === undefined) out.push({ path: ["rowActions"], message: "rowActions need rowKeys" });
    if (rowActions.length !== rows.length) out.push({ path: ["rowActions"], message: "rowActions must have one list per row" });
    rowActions.forEach((list, r) => {
      if (!Array.isArray(list)) return;
      const at = (message: string): void => void out.push({ path: ["rowActions", r], message });
      if (list.length > MAX_ROW_ACTIONS) at(`a row has at most ${MAX_ROW_ACTIONS} actions`);
      const ids = list.flatMap((a) => (isRecord(a) && typeof a.id === "string" ? [a.id] : []));
      if (new Set(ids).size !== ids.length) at("action ids must be unique within a row");
      if (list.filter((a) => isRecord(a) && a.primary === true).length > 1) at("a row has at most one primary action");
      for (const a of list) {
        if (!isRecord(a)) continue;
        if (typeof a.id === "string" && !ACTION_ID.test(a.id)) at("an action id must be lower-case words");
        if (typeof a.label === "string" && (a.label.length < 1 || a.label.length > 40)) at("an action label is 1 to 40 characters");
        if (a.primary === true && a.destructive === true) at("a destructive action cannot be primary");
      }
    });
  }
  return out;
}
```

  `values.ts`: add the schema and extend `TableValue`:

```ts
import { tableRowProblems } from "./tableRows";

export const TableRowAction = z.object({
  id: z.string(),
  label: z.string(),
  primary: z.boolean().optional(),
  destructive: z.boolean().optional(),
});
export const TableValue = z
  .object({
    columns: z.array(TableColumn),
    rows: z.array(z.array(TableCell)),
    rowKeys: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
    rowActions: z.array(z.array(TableRowAction)).optional(),
  })
  .superRefine((v, ctx) => {
    for (const p of tableRowProblems(v)) ctx.addIssue({ code: z.ZodIssueCode.custom, path: p.path, message: p.message });
  });
export type TableValue = z.infer<typeof TableValue>;
```

  (If anything in the package reads `TableValue.shape`, `grep -rn "TableValue\." packages/*/src` finds it; use `TableValue.innerType().shape` there.)

  `kindValues.ts`: replace `table` with the twin:

```ts
import { tableRowProblems } from "./tableRows";

const optionalBoolean = (v: unknown, p: Path, out: Out): Status => opt(v, p, out, { optional: true }, (x, xp, xo) => type("boolean", x, xp, xo));
const rowAction = (v: unknown, p: Path, out: Out): Status =>
  object(v, p, out, [["id", string], ["label", string], ["primary", optionalBoolean], ["destructive", optionalBoolean]]);

const table = (v: unknown, p: Path, out: Out): Status => {
  const status = object(v, p, out, [
    ["columns", (c, cp, co) => array(c, cp, co, tableColumn)],
    ["rows", (r, rp, ro) => array(r, rp, ro, (row, rwp, rwo) => array(row, rwp, rwo, tableCell))],
    ["rowKeys", (k, kp, ko) => opt(k, kp, ko, { optional: true }, (x, xp, xo) => array(x, xp, xo, string))],
    ["rowActions", (a, ap, ao) => opt(a, ap, ao, { optional: true }, (x, xp, xo) => array(x, xp, xo, (r, rp, ro) => array(r, rp, ro, rowAction)))],
  ]);
  if (status === "aborted") return status;
  let worst: Status = status;
  for (const problem of tableRowProblems(v as { rows: readonly unknown[]; rowKeys?: unknown; rowActions?: unknown })) {
    for (const seg of problem.path) p.push(seg);
    fail(out, p, problem.message);
    p.length -= problem.path.length;
    worst = "dirty";
  }
  return worst;
};
```

Add the new valid and invalid values from the test above to the differential fixtures.

### Task 176b (loom-ui repo): `Chat` inline reference markers

**Repo and branch:** `~/workspace/loom-ui`, branch `feat/chat-reference-markers`, a draft PR, rebase-merged. Same conventions as Task 176a. It can run in parallel with 176a (different files except `messages.ts`, `index.ts` and `browser.ts`/`chatUi.ts` exports: rebase over whichever lands first).

**Why (generic).** A transcript source that cites (retrieval answers, support replies that point at tickets, research notes with footnotes) needs a marker inside a sentence that the reader can activate to see the source. `Chat` draws text only and must keep doing so (no markup in text), but it can draw a *button* for a marker the host listed, and report the activation. The marker syntax is `[[id]]` inside a text part; the host lists the ids an entry may cite in `refs`; anything else stays text-safe: an unlisted marker is removed, and while a reply streams every marker is hidden (the citations are known only when the reply is durable).

**Contract.**
- `ChatEntry.refs?: readonly ChatRef[]` (`ChatRef = { id }`, `id` matches the entry id pattern, at most `MAX_CHAT_REFS = 64`, unique; only on an assistant entry; refused on a restricted entry like every content field). `ChatValue.refMarkers?: boolean`: the source writes markers; only then does the widget process them (a host that never sets it sees today's behavior, text exactly as sent).
- `splitRefMarkers(text, known, { hideAll })` (zod-free, in `@loom-ui/core/chat-ui`): returns text and `ref` segments; a marker whose id is in `known` becomes `{ type: "ref", id, n }` numbered by first appearance of the id; every other marker is removed together with one space before it; with `hideAll` every marker and a trailing partial `[[...` are removed.
- The widget draws each `ref` segment as `<button type="button" class="ref" data-ref="<id>" aria-label="Source <n>">n</button>` and emits `{ type: "CHAT_REF_OPEN", widgetId, payload: { entryId, refId, n } }` (a read, like `LOAD_OLDER`: no key, never routed to a write target).
- `ChatFeed({ refMarkers: true })` sets the value's flag and folds a wire field `refs: string[]` (ids) into the entry's `refs`.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/core/src/chatRefs.ts`, `packages/core/src/chatRefs.test.ts`, `packages/renderer-lit/src/widgets/chatRefs.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/core/src/chatValue.ts`, `packages/core/src/chatActions.ts`, `packages/core/src/chatFeed.ts`, `packages/core/src/chatUi.ts`, `packages/core/src/chatHost.ts` (the action-to-kind table), `packages/core/src/index.ts`, `packages/core/src/messages.ts`, `packages/renderer-lit/src/widgets/lu-chat.ts`, `packages/core/README.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION = "CHAT_REF_OPEN"`; `MAX_CHAT_REFS`; `ChatRef`; `ChatEntry.refs`, `ChatValue.refMarkers`; `splitRefMarkers`; `RefSegment`; message key `lu.chat.ref` ("Source {n}"); `ChatFeed` option `refMarkers`.

`packages/core/src/chatRefs.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { ChatFeed } from "./chatFeed";
import { checkKindValue } from "./browser";
import { CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION, splitRefMarkers } from "./chatRefs";

const ids = new Set(["a1", "b2"]);
const seg = (text: string, o = { hideAll: false }) => splitRefMarkers(text, ids, o);

describe("splitRefMarkers", () => {
  it("names the action", () => expect(CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION).toBe("CHAT_REF_OPEN"));
  it("turns a listed marker into a numbered reference, by first appearance of the id", () => {
    expect(seg("Due in 30 days [[b2]] and net [[a1]], again [[b2]].")).toEqual([
      { type: "text", text: "Due in 30 days " }, { type: "ref", id: "b2", n: 1 },
      { type: "text", text: " and net " }, { type: "ref", id: "a1", n: 2 },
      { type: "text", text: ", again " }, { type: "ref", id: "b2", n: 1 }, { type: "text", text: "." },
    ]);
  });
  it("removes a marker that is not listed, with the one space before it", () => {
    expect(seg("Net 30 [[zzz]].")).toEqual([{ type: "text", text: "Net 30." }]);
    expect(seg("[[zzz]]x")).toEqual([{ type: "text", text: "x" }]);
  });
  it("leaves text that merely looks like a marker alone (spaces, empty, too long, wrong characters)", () => {
    const odd = ["[[]]", "[[ a1 ]]", "[a1]", `[[${"a".repeat(129)}]]`, "[[a b]]"];
    for (const t of odd) expect(seg(t)).toEqual([{ type: "text", text: t }]);
  });
  it("hides every marker, and a marker that has not finished arriving, while streaming", () => {
    expect(seg("Due [[a1]] in 30 [[b2", { hideAll: true })).toEqual([{ type: "text", text: "Due in 30" }]);
    expect(seg("Due [[", { hideAll: true })).toEqual([{ type: "text", text: "Due" }]);
    expect(seg("Due [", { hideAll: true })).toEqual([{ type: "text", text: "Due [" }]);
  });
  it("is linear on adversarial input", () => {
    const t = "[[".repeat(50_000);
    const start = Date.now();
    splitRefMarkers(t, ids, { hideAll: true });
    expect(Date.now() - start).toBeLessThan(500);
  });
});

describe("refs on an entry and the feed", () => {
  const entry = (over: Record<string, unknown>) => ({ entries: [{ id: "t1.assistant", turn: 1, role: "assistant", status: "complete", parts: [{ type: "text", text: "x [[a1]]" }], ...over }], refMarkers: true });
  it("accepts a list of unique ids on an assistant entry", () => expect(checkKindValue("Chat", entry({ refs: [{ id: "a1" }, { id: "b2" }] })).ok).toBe(true));
  it.each([
    ["a duplicate id", entry({ refs: [{ id: "a1" }, { id: "a1" }] })],
    ["a bad id", entry({ refs: [{ id: "a b" }] })],
    ["too many", entry({ refs: Array.from({ length: 65 }, (_, i) => ({ id: `r${i}` })) })],
    ["refs on a user entry", { entries: [{ id: "m1", turn: 1, role: "user", status: "complete", refs: [{ id: "a1" }] }] }],
    ["refs on a restricted entry", entry({ status: "restricted", parts: undefined, refs: [{ id: "a1" }] })],
  ])("refuses %s", (_n, v) => expect(checkKindValue("Chat", v).ok).toBe(false));

  it("folds a wire refs field into the entry when the feed is told the source writes markers", () => {
    const feed = new ChatFeed({ refMarkers: true });
    feed.applyHistory({ entries: [{ turn: 1, role: "assistant", status: "complete", text: "x [[a1]]", cursor: "1.1", refs: ["a1", "bad id", "a1"] }], done: true }, "backward");
    const v = feed.value();
    expect(v.refMarkers).toBe(true);
    expect(v.entries[0]?.refs).toEqual([{ id: "a1" }]);
    expect(new ChatFeed().value().refMarkers).toBeUndefined();
  });
});
```

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import "../elements/lu-chat";

}

    const buttons = [...art(el).querySelectorAll<HTMLButtonElement>("button.ref")];
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the core half.**

`packages/core/src/chatRefs.ts` (zod-free, DOM-free):

```ts
// ── Inline reference markers (U8b) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// A transcript source that cites writes `[[id]]` in a text part and lists the ids an entry may cite in `refs`.
// This is NOT markup: a marker is a token the WIDGET replaces with a button it builds itself, and only for an id the
// host listed. Unlisted markers are removed; while a reply streams every marker is hidden (the cited set is known only
// when the reply is durable).

export const CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION = "CHAT_REF_OPEN";
export const MAX_CHAT_REFS = 64;

export type RefSegment =
  | { readonly type: "text"; readonly text: string }
  | { readonly type: "ref"; readonly id: string; readonly n: number };

const MARKER = /\[\[([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127})\]\]/g;
// `[[`, then up to 128 id characters, then at most one `]`, at the very end: a marker still arriving.
const PARTIAL = /\[\[[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,128}\]?$/;

export function splitRefMarkers(text: string, known: ReadonlySet<string>, o: { readonly hideAll: boolean }): RefSegment[] {
  const out: RefSegment[] = [];
  const numbers = new Map<string, number>();
  let buf = "";
  const flush = (): void => {
    if (buf !== "") out.push({ type: "text", text: buf });
    buf = "";
  };
  let last = 0;
  for (const m of text.matchAll(MARKER)) {
    const at = m.index as number;
    buf += text.slice(last, at);
    last = at + m[0].length;
    const id = m[1] as string;
    if (o.hideAll || !known.has(id)) {
      if (buf.endsWith(" ")) buf = buf.slice(0, -1);
      continue;
    }
    let n = numbers.get(id);
    if (n === undefined) {
      n = numbers.size + 1;
      numbers.set(id, n);
    }
    flush();
    out.push({ type: "ref", id, n });
  }
  buf += text.slice(last);
  if (o.hideAll) {
    const tail = PARTIAL.exec(buf);
    if (tail) {
      buf = buf.slice(0, tail.index);
      if (buf.endsWith(" ")) buf = buf.slice(0, -1);
    }
  }
  flush();
  return out;
}
```

  (`MARKER` is global and `matchAll` clones it, so no state leaks. Both patterns are linear: the id classes are bounded and anchored by `]]`/end.)

  `chatActions.ts`: re-export `CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION` beside the other action constants (import from `./chatRefs`) and document it in the header comment: `CHAT_REF_OPEN { entryId, refId, n }  a Chat: the user opened a reference marker (a read; carries no key)`. `chatHost.ts`: add `[CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION]: "Chat"` to `TYPE_TO_KIND` next to `LOAD_OLDER_ACTION` and treat it as a `browser-side` read exactly as `LOAD_OLDER` is. `chatUi.ts` and `index.ts`: export `splitRefMarkers`, `RefSegment`, `CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION`, `MAX_CHAT_REFS`.

  `chatValue.ts`:
  - `export interface ChatRef { readonly id: string }`; in `ChatEntry`: `/** Ids the entry's text may cite as [[id]] markers (an assistant entry; the source sets ChatValue.refMarkers). */ readonly refs?: readonly ChatRef[];`; in `ChatValue`: `readonly refMarkers?: boolean;`.
  - add `"refs"` to `ENTRY_KEYS` and `"refMarkers"` to `VALUE_KEYS`.
  - in `entryIssues` (after `tools`): `refs` must be an array of at most `MAX_CHAT_REFS` records `{ id }` with `id` matching `ID`, unique, on an `assistant` entry only: messages `refs must be a list`, `at most 64 refs`, `a ref id must be a short identifier`, `ref ids must be unique`, `only an assistant entry has refs`.
  - where the restricted placeholder refuses `parts`, `tools`, `reason`, `errorId` and `editOf`, refuse `refs` too (same message style).
  - `refMarkers`, when present, must be a boolean (`refMarkers must be true or false`).

  `chatFeed.ts`: `ChatFeedOptions` gains `readonly refMarkers?: boolean`; `chatEntryFromWire` reads `refs` (an array; keep the strings that pass `isChatEntryId`, drop duplicates, cap at `MAX_CHAT_REFS`) and sets `refs: ids.map((id) => ({ id }))` when non-empty and the entry is a non-bare assistant entry; `value()` adds `refMarkers: true` when the option is set.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the widget.** In `lu-chat.ts`: import `CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION`, `splitRefMarkers` from `@loom-ui/core/chat-ui`; in the text-part branch of the entry template replace `${cleanText(p)}` with `${this.textOf(e, p)}`:

```ts
  /** The text part as text, with listed reference markers drawn as buttons when the source writes markers. */
  private textOf(e: ChatEntry, p: ChatPart & { type: "text" }): TemplateResult | string {
    const clean = cleanText(p);
    const v = this.value as ChatValue;
    if (v.refMarkers !== true || e.role !== "assistant") return clean;
    const known = new Set((e.refs ?? []).map((r) => r.id));
    const parts = splitRefMarkers(clean, known, { hideAll: e.status === "streaming" });
    return html`${parts.map((s) =>
      s.type === "text"
        ? s.text
        : html`<button type="button" class="ref" data-ref=${s.id} aria-label=${say("lu.chat.ref", { n: s.n })} @click=${() => this.openRef(e.id, s.id, s.n)}>${s.n}</button>`,
    )}`;
  }

  private openRef(entryId: string, refId: string, n: number): void {
    void dispatch(this.onAction, { type: CHAT_REF_OPEN_ACTION, widgetId: this.widget.id, payload: { entryId, refId, n } });
  }
```

  and the style: `button.ref { all: unset; cursor: pointer; display: inline-block; min-width: 1.4em; margin: 0 1px; padding: 0 4px; text-align: center; font-size: 0.8em; line-height: 1.5; vertical-align: super; border-radius: var(--lu-radius, 6px); background: var(--lu-panel-2, rgba(99, 102, 241, 0.16)); color: var(--lu-accent, #6366f1); } button.ref:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--lu-accent, #6366f1); outline-offset: 1px; }`. `messages.ts`: `"lu.chat.ref": "Source {n}"`. The cached `cleanText` stays as it is; `textOf` is not cached (cheap, bounded by `MAX_CHAT_TEXT`).

Expected: PASS. **Probe.** The conformance kit's "untrusted text" check must stay green (it proves nothing becomes markup); if a bundle budget moves, raise it by the measured amount and say so in the PR.

```bash
git commit -m "feat(core,renderer-lit): Chat inline reference markers (refs, [[id]], CHAT_REF_OPEN)
```

**PR body:** "A transcript can cite. An assistant entry lists the ids it may cite in `refs`; text parts carry `[[id]]` markers; `lu-chat` draws each listed marker as a numbered button and reports `CHAT_REF_OPEN { entryId, refId, n }`. Unlisted markers are removed, every marker is hidden while the reply streams, and nothing in the text becomes markup. Opt-in per source (`ChatFeed({ refMarkers: true })`); without it the widget is unchanged. Example: a support transcript citing ticket numbers." (and the generated-with line).

### Markdown in answers: the loom-ui design (Tasks 176c to 176k)

Owner decision (binding, replaces the earlier default of open question 4): **answers render markdown, and it is a loom-ui-first, generic change.** loom-ui's `Chat` refuses markup today on purpose ("everything is text"); that guarantee is what lets a transcript hold model output safely. Markdown is added so the guarantee survives: it is **opt-in per text part, parsed into a closed and bounded tree, and drawn by building DOM nodes**; the default (a part with no `format`) is byte-for-byte what it is today. The text of the eight loom-ui PRs names no consumer; examples are support tickets and refunds. DocMind's part is small (Task 193 and the acceptance test); the rest is loom-ui.

| # | Question | Decision and why |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | **Unit of opt-in** | A text part says `format: "markdown"` (absent or `"plain"` = as typed). Allowed on an **assistant** entry only: a person's own words are shown as they typed them (and an edit round-trips them). The source opts in once with `ChatFeed({ markdown: true })`, which marks the text parts of assistant entries, the live reply included. The contract (`checkKindValue`) refuses any other `format` value, markdown on a user entry, and a stray field next to it. |
| M2 | **Subset** | Paragraphs; ATX headings; emphasis and strong; inline code; fenced code blocks; bullet and ordered lists (nested); blockquotes; thematic breaks; `[label](url)` links; hard breaks; **GFM tables**; and the `[[id]]` reference markers of Task 176b. Tables are in because they are the one structure plain text cannot carry and answers about documents compare and list data constantly; they cost about 60 lines, are bounded (12 columns, 100 rows) and are drawn as a real `<table>`. **Out:** raw HTML (`<b>` is shown as the characters), entities (`&amp;` stays), images, autolinks and bare URLs (never linked), reference-style links, setext headings, indented code, footnotes, strikethrough, task lists. Backslash escapes work on ASCII punctuation. The subset is documented as a subset: it does not claim CommonMark conformance. |
| M3 | **Parser: hand-written, no dependency** | loom-ui's dependency rules (`AGENTS.md`: third-party versions only through `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `catalog:`; `@loom-ui/core` is pure and isomorphic; the `chat-ui` entry is zod-free, DOM-free and ships to every browser bundle) make a new runtime dependency expensive, and none of the usual parsers fits the need: the common ones produce an HTML string (`marked`, `markdown-it` with its `html` flag), or an open node set (`mdast`/`remark`: dozens of node types including raw HTML, images and definitions, several packages, unbounded depth). Whatever parser is chosen, the work that carries the safety guarantee still has to be written and tested around it: an allow-list over the output, the link rule, node, depth and time bounds, streaming behavior, marker handling. The subset itself is about 600 lines including those. So the parser is written here, as pure functions over a closed tree, with adversarial tests pinning absolute time bounds. **Trade-off accepted:** edge-case conformance bugs are loom-ui's to fix (mitigated: it is a documented subset, and nothing outside the subset can reach a renderer). *Alternative:* pin `commonmark` exactly through the catalog and wrap it in the same allow-list; revisit only if the subset has to grow. |
| M4 | **Never HTML** | The source is cleaned with `sanitizeChatText` first (controls, bidi, TAG characters, the 64 KiB cut). The parser has no HTML path at all. The renderer builds Lit templates whose dynamic values are text bindings and a few bounded attribute bindings; **no `unsafeHTML`, `innerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `style` attribute or event attribute**, scanned for by a test, and a hostile-corpus test walks the rendered DOM for any tag, attribute or address outside a closed list. The AST is never part of a value: every renderer parses the text itself, so a source cannot forge a `link` node. |
| M5 | **Links** | `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:` only; no whitespace, control character, backslash or userinfo (`https://a@b`); at most 2048 characters; no relative or `#fragment` links (a transcript has no base). Anything else is dropped and the label stays as text. Drawn as `<a href title=href target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow" referrerpolicy="no-referrer">`: a new context so the transcript stays open, no opener, no referrer, and the address is the `title` so a label cannot hide it. The rule runs in the parser **and again** in the renderer. |
| M6 | **Images** | None. A markdown image is the classic exfiltration and tracking channel (the address can carry data and is fetched on render); real media have their own contract (`media` parts, a host-resolved address). The alt text is shown as text. |
| M7 | **Bounds** | 2000 nodes in all (the rest is cut, `truncated`), block nesting 6, inline nesting 4, 200 emphasis delimiter runs per inline run (more are literal), tables 12 x 100, URL 2048, text 64 KiB (the existing cap). Every scan is a single pass or a pre-indexed lookup; the tests pin absolute bounds (1.5 s) on 30 to 60 thousand characters of adversarial input per construct. |
| M8 | **Composes with `[[id]]` markers** | The parser emits `marker` nodes only when the value says `refMarkers`, never inside a code span, a code block or a link label (a button inside an anchor would be nested interaction). `resolveMdMarkers` then applies 176b's rules to the tree (numbered by first appearance in reading order, unlisted removed with one space before, **every marker hidden while the reply streams**, a trailing half-written `[[id` removed). A renderer draws `ref` nodes and never an unresolved `marker`. A marker inside emphasis, a list item or a table cell works. |
| M9 | **Streaming** | Parsing is a pure function of the text so far, so a half-written reply is a shorter text and the same code settles it: an unclosed fence is code to the end, an unmatched `*` or backtick is literal, a half-written `[label](ur` shows its label only, a half-written `[[id` shows nothing. The durable message replaces the live text in the same slot (the renderer keeps `t<turn>.assistant`), so nothing remounts. Honest limit: `**bold` shows the asterisks until the closer arrives. |
| M10 | **Terminal** | The same tree is drawn as plain lines by `chatMarkdownLines` (core, so any renderer without a DOM can use it): headings keep `#`, emphasis keeps its `*`, a link prints as `label (address)` (a terminal cannot hide where a link goes), a marker as `[n]`, a table in pipe form, code lines under `| `. **No ANSI**: `renderExperience` makes the frame inert (no ESC, C0, C1 or bidi) and its snapshot output must stay byte-identical; styling would be a separate opt-in. |
| M11 | **Conformance and docs** | `@loom-ui/renderer-conformance` gets a `chat-markdown` check every renderer must pass (html literal, no unsafe link target, no image address, a plain part never parsed, a user turn's markdown refused; with the `chatMarkdown` capability, the markdown source is not shown) and a fault for each clause in `speech.test.ts`. The core README, AGENTS.md, the renderer guide and a reference snippet are updated: "no markdown" becomes "markdown only when the source asks, as a closed tree". |
| M12 | **Accessibility** | Native lists, `<table>` with `scope="col"` headers inside a focusable, named region (keyboard scroll), `<pre tabindex=0>` for code, `<blockquote>`, links as anchors. Headings are `role="heading"` with `aria-level` offset by two (`#` is level 3, the deepest is 6): a transcript sits below the page's own headings and must not rewrite its outline. |

**Order inside loom-ui:** 176b (markers) first; then 176c to 176g (the parser, core only, nothing user-visible); 176h (the contract and the feed option); 176i (the web renderer); 176j (shared plain-text lines and the terminal); 176k (conformance, docs, reference snippet). Each is a draft PR cut from the previous one; DocMind's Task 177b pins the merge of the last.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.** `packages/core/src/chatMdTypes.ts`:

```ts
// ── Markdown in a chat transcript: the AST, its bounds and the link rule (U8c) ──────────────────────
// See `chatMarkdown.ts` for the whole contract. Zod-free and DOM-free; nothing here produces HTML.

export const MD_MAX_NODES = 2000;
export const MD_MAX_DEPTH = 6;
export const MD_MAX_INLINE_DEPTH = 4;
export const MD_MAX_TABLE_COLS = 12;
export const MD_MAX_TABLE_ROWS = 100;
export const MD_MAX_URL = 2048;
export type MdAlign = "left" | "center" | "right" | null;
export type MdInline =
  | { readonly type: "text"; readonly text: string }
  | { readonly type: "code"; readonly text: string }
  | { readonly type: "em"; readonly children: readonly MdInline[] }
  | { readonly type: "strong"; readonly children: readonly MdInline[] }
  | { readonly type: "link"; readonly href: string; readonly children: readonly MdInline[] }
  | { readonly type: "br" }
  /** A `[[id]]` marker as parsed: not yet checked against the entry's `refs`. A renderer never draws it. */
  | { readonly type: "marker"; readonly id: string }
  /** A marker the entry listed, numbered by first appearance ({@link resolveMdMarkers}). */
  | { readonly type: "ref"; readonly id: string; readonly n: number };
export type MdBlock =
  | { readonly type: "paragraph"; readonly inlines: readonly MdInline[] }
  | {
      readonly type: "heading";
      readonly level: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6;
      readonly inlines: readonly MdInline[];
    }
  | { readonly type: "code"; readonly lang?: string; readonly text: string }
  | { readonly type: "quote"; readonly blocks: readonly MdBlock[] }
  | {
      readonly type: "list";
      readonly ordered: boolean;
      readonly start: number;
      readonly items: readonly (readonly MdBlock[])[];
    }
  | {
      readonly type: "table";
      readonly align: readonly MdAlign[];
      readonly head: readonly (readonly MdInline[])[];
      readonly rows: readonly (readonly (readonly MdInline[])[])[];
    }
  | { readonly type: "rule" };
export interface MdDoc {
  readonly blocks: readonly MdBlock[];
  /** A bound cut the document short. */
  readonly truncated: boolean;
}
export interface ChatMarkdownOptions {
  /** The source writes `[[id]]` markers (`ChatValue.refMarkers`). Off: `[[id]]` is ordinary text. */
  readonly markers?: boolean;
  /** The reply is still arriving. */
  readonly streaming?: boolean;
}

export interface MdState {
  nodes: number;
  truncated: boolean;
  readonly markers: boolean;
  readonly streaming: boolean;
}
export const take = (st: MdState): boolean => {
  if (st.nodes >= MD_MAX_NODES) {
    st.truncated = true;
    return false;
  }
  st.nodes++;
  return true;
};

// ── links ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/** `raw` if it is a link a transcript may carry (http, https, mailto; no whitespace, controls, backslash or userinfo), else undefined. */
export function safeLinkHref(raw: string): string | undefined {
  if (raw.length === 0 || raw.length > MD_MAX_URL) return undefined;
  for (let k = 0; k < raw.length; k++) {
    const c = raw.charCodeAt(k);
    if (c <= 0x20 || (c >= 0x7f && c <= 0x9f) || c === 0x5c) return undefined;
  }
  const lower = raw.slice(0, 8).toLowerCase();
  if (lower.startsWith("mailto:")) return raw.length > 7 ? raw : undefined;
  const web = lower.startsWith("https://") ? 8 : lower.startsWith("http://") ? 7 : 0;
  if (web === 0) return undefined;
  let end = web;
  while (end < raw.length && raw[end] !== "/" && raw[end] !== "?" && raw[end] !== "#") end++;
  const authority = raw.slice(web, end);
  return authority === "" || authority.includes("@") ? undefined : raw;
}

/** The text of inline nodes with no markup (an image's alt text, an accessible name). */
export function mdPlainText(nodes: readonly MdInline[]): string {
  let out = "";
  for (const n of nodes) {
    if (n.type === "text" || n.type === "code") out += n.text;
    else if (n.type === "br") out += " ";
    else if (n.type === "em" || n.type === "strong" || n.type === "link")
      out += mdPlainText(n.children);
  }
  return out;
}
```

  `packages/core/src/chatMdScan.ts` (each helper is one pass, or a pre-built index, so the inline parser of 176d stays linear):

```ts
// ── Markdown scanning helpers: reference markers, code-span runs, link destinations (U8c) ──────────
// Each is a single pass (or a pre-indexed lookup), so the inline parser stays linear.

const MAX_ID = 128;
const ID_CHAR = /[A-Za-z0-9._:-]/;

export function markerAt(s: string, at: number): { id: string; end: number } | undefined {
  if (s[at] !== "[" || s[at + 1] !== "[" || !/[A-Za-z0-9]/.test(s[at + 2] ?? "")) return undefined;
  let k = at + 2;
  while (k < s.length && k - at - 2 < MAX_ID && ID_CHAR.test(s[k] as string)) k++;
  return s[k] === "]" && s[k + 1] === "]" ? { id: s.slice(at + 2, k), end: k + 2 } : undefined;
}

/** Index of every backtick run, and for each the next run of the SAME length: code spans in O(n). */
export function tickRuns(s: string): { pos: number; len: number; next: number }[] {
  const runs: { pos: number; len: number; next: number }[] = [];
  for (let k = 0; k < s.length; ) {
    if (s[k] !== "`") {
      k++;
      continue;
    }
    const pos = k;
    while (s[k] === "`") k++;
    runs.push({ pos, len: k - pos, next: -1 });
  }
  const last = new Map<number, number>();
  for (let r = runs.length - 1; r >= 0; r--) {
    const run = runs[r] as { len: number; next: number };
    run.next = last.get(run.len) ?? -1;
    last.set(run.len, r);
  }
  return runs;
}

/** The destination of `(dest "title")` starting at `src[at] === "("`: its end (after the `)`), or undefined; `eof` when the text ended first. */
export function destAt(src: string, at: number): { href: string; end: number } | "eof" | undefined {
  let k = at + 1;
  while (src[k] === " ") k++;
  let href: string;
  if (src[k] === "<") {
    const close = src.indexOf(">", k);
    if (close < 0 || src.slice(k, close).includes("\n"))
      return close < 0 && !src.includes("\n", k) ? "eof" : undefined;
    href = src.slice(k + 1, close);
    k = close + 1;
  } else {
    const from = k;
    let depth = 0;
    while (k < src.length && src[k] !== " " && src[k] !== "\n") {
      if (src[k] === "\\" && k + 1 < src.length) k++;
      else if (src[k] === "(") depth++;
      else if (src[k] === ")") {
        if (depth === 0) break;
        depth--;
      }
      k++;
    }
    href = src.slice(from, k).replace(/\\([!-/:-@[-`{-~])/g, "$1");
  }
  while (src[k] === " ") k++;
  if (src[k] === '"' || src[k] === "'") {
    const q = src[k] as string;
    const close = src.indexOf(q, k + 1);
    if (close < 0) return "eof";
    k = close + 1;
    while (src[k] === " ") k++;
  }
  if (k >= src.length) return "eof";
  return src[k] === ")" ? { href, end: k + 1 } : undefined;
}
```

### Task 176d (loom-ui repo): markdown 2 of 9: inline parsing (code spans, escapes, links, images, markers, streaming)

**Repo and branch:** `~/workspace/loom-ui`, branch `feat/chat-md-inline`, cut from 176c, a draft PR, rebase-merged, stacked in the order of the task numbers (176b first). Same conventions as Task 176a: read loom-ui's `AGENTS.md` first (Loom is consumed built; `typecheck:tests` is a gate). The gate is `pnpm build && pnpm typecheck && pnpm typecheck:tests && pnpm test && pnpm lint:fix && pnpm lint` (build first: renderer packages resolve `@loom-ui/core` from its built `dist`). 

**Why (generic).** The inline half of the parser: everything inside a paragraph except emphasis (176e). It is where untrusted text meets the allowlist, so it is its own PR.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/core/src/chatMdInline.ts`, `packages/core/src/chatMdInline.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: 176c's helpers and tree.
- Produces: `parseInlines(source, state): MdInline[]` (internal): text, `code`, `link`, `br`, `marker`; an image becomes its alt text; an unmatched bracket or backtick is literal; `streaming` drops a trailing half-written `[[id` (when markers are on) and shows only the label of a half-written link; every node is charged to the budget and the rest is cut.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** `packages/core/src/chatMdInline.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { parseInlines } from "./chatMdInline";
import { MD_MAX_NODES, type MdInline, mdPlainText } from "./chatMdTypes";

const inl = (t: string, o: { markers?: boolean; streaming?: boolean } = {}): readonly MdInline[] =>
  parseInlines(t, {
    nodes: 0,
    truncated: false,
    markers: o.markers === true,
    streaming: o.streaming === true,
  });
const txt = (text: string): MdInline => ({ type: "text", text });

describe("code spans", () => {
  it("takes no markup inside a code span, and strips one padding space", () => {
    expect(inl("`**a** [[a1]] <b>`", { markers: true })).toEqual([
      { type: "code", text: "**a** [[a1]] <b>" },
    ]);
    expect(inl("`` a`b ``")).toEqual([{ type: "code", text: "a`b" }]);
  });
  it("leaves an unmatched backtick run as the literal characters", () => {
    expect(inl("open `tick and ``two")).toEqual([txt("open `tick and ``two")]);
  });
});

describe("escapes, breaks, html", () => {
  it("handles backslash escapes and hard breaks, and keeps a soft break", () => {
    expect(inl("\\*a\\* \\q")).toEqual([txt("*a* \\q")]);
    expect(inl("a  \nb")).toEqual([txt("a"), { type: "br" }, txt("b")]);
    expect(inl("a\\\nb")).toEqual([txt("a"), { type: "br" }, txt("b")]);
    expect(inl("a\nb")).toEqual([txt("a\nb")]);
  });
  it("never reads html or entities", () => {
    const t = "<b>x</b> &amp; <script>alert(1)</script> <img src=x onerror=1>";
    expect(inl(t)).toEqual([txt(t)]);
  });
});

describe("links and images", () => {
  it("makes a link only for an allowed address", () => {
    expect(inl('[docs](https://example.com/a?b=1 "t")')).toEqual([
      { type: "link", href: "https://example.com/a?b=1", children: [txt("docs")] },
    ]);
    expect(inl("[**m**](mailto:a@b.example)")[0]).toMatchObject({
      type: "link",
      href: "mailto:a@b.example",
    });
    expect(inl("[x](https://a.example/(p))")[0]).toMatchObject({ href: "https://a.example/(p)" });
  });
  it.each([
    "javascript:alert(1)",
    "JaVaScRiPt:x",
    "data:text/html,x",
    "//evil.example",
    "/rel",
    "#frag",
    "https://u:p@evil.example/",
    "java\tscript:x",
    "file:///etc/passwd",
  ])("keeps only the label for %j", (bad) => {
    const out = inl(`[click](${bad})`);
    expect(out.map((n) => n.type)).not.toContain("link");
    expect(mdPlainText(out)).toBe("click");
  });
  it("never links a bare address or a bracket without one, and never nests links", () => {
    expect(inl("see https://example.com bare")).toEqual([txt("see https://example.com bare")]);
    expect(inl("[x]")).toEqual([txt("[x]")]);
    expect(
      inl("[a [b](https://x.example) c](https://y.example)").filter((n) => n.type === "link")
    ).toHaveLength(1);
  });
  it("shows an image as its alt text and carries no address", () => {
    expect(inl("![a chart](https://tracker.example/p.png)")).toEqual([txt("a chart")]);
    expect(JSON.stringify(inl("![x](https://tracker.example/p.png?d=secret)"))).not.toContain(
      "tracker"
    );
  });
});

describe("reference markers", () => {
  it("are text unless the source writes them, never inside code, and not inside a link label", () => {
    expect(inl("a [[a1]] b")).toEqual([txt("a [[a1]] b")]);
    expect(inl("a [[a1]] b", { markers: true })).toEqual([
      txt("a "),
      { type: "marker", id: "a1" },
      txt(" b"),
    ]);
    expect(inl("x [[a_b.c:d-e]]", { markers: true })).toEqual([
      txt("x "),
      { type: "marker", id: "a_b.c:d-e" },
    ]);
    const link = inl("[see [[a1]]](https://x.example)", { markers: true })[0];
    expect(link).toMatchObject({ type: "link" });
    expect(JSON.stringify(link)).not.toContain("marker");
    expect(inl("[[ a1 ]] [[]] [[a b]]", { markers: true }).every((n) => n.type !== "marker")).toBe(
      true
    );
  });
});

describe("streaming", () => {
  it("does not draw a half-written marker", () => {
    expect(inl("Due [[a1", { markers: true, streaming: true })).toEqual([txt("Due")]);
    expect(inl("Due [[", { markers: true, streaming: true })).toEqual([txt("Due")]);
    expect(inl("Due [[a1]", { markers: true, streaming: true })).toEqual([txt("Due")]);
    expect(inl("Due [", { markers: true, streaming: true })).toEqual([txt("Due [")]);
    expect(inl("Due [[a1", { markers: true })).toEqual([txt("Due [[a1")]);
  });
  it("draws only the label of a half-written link, then the link when it is whole", () => {
    for (const t of [
      "see [the doc",
      "see [the doc]",
      "see [the doc](http",
      "see [the doc](https://exam",
    ]) {
      const s = mdPlainText(inl(t, { streaming: true }));
      expect(s.includes("](") || s.includes("http"), t).toBe(false);
    }
    expect(mdPlainText(inl("see [the doc]", { streaming: true }))).toBe("see the doc");
    expect(mdPlainText(inl("see [the doc]", { streaming: false }))).toBe("see [the doc]");
    expect(inl("see [the doc](https://e.example)", { streaming: true })[1]).toMatchObject({
      type: "link",
    });
  });
  it("settles: every prefix parses, and the whole text equals the settled parse", () => {
    const full = "Some **bold** and `code` [[a1]] and [l](https://e.example) ok.";
    for (let k = 0; k <= full.length; k++)
      expect(() => inl(full.slice(0, k), { markers: true, streaming: true })).not.toThrow();
    expect(inl(full, { markers: true, streaming: true })).toEqual(inl(full, { markers: true }));
  });
});

describe("bounds", () => {
  it("is linear on adversarial input", () => {
    const cases = [
      "*a ".repeat(30000),
      "[".repeat(60000),
      "`a ".repeat(20000),
      "[[".repeat(30000),
      "_*".repeat(30000),
      "[a](".repeat(15000),
      "![".repeat(30000),
      "a  \n".repeat(15000),
      "**".repeat(30000),
      "((((".repeat(15000),
      "[a](<".repeat(10000),
      "*a*".repeat(20000),
      "[a]".repeat(20000),
    ];
    for (const c of cases) {
      const t = Date.now();
      inl(c, { markers: true, streaming: true });
      expect(Date.now() - t, c.slice(0, 6)).toBeLessThan(1500);
    }
  });
  it("charges every node to the budget and cuts the rest", () => {
    const st = { nodes: 0, truncated: false, markers: false, streaming: false };
    const out = parseInlines("`a` ".repeat(5000), st);
    expect(st.truncated).toBe(true);
    expect(out.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(MD_MAX_NODES);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to see it fail.** `pnpm --filter @loom-ui/core exec vitest run src/chatMdInline.test.ts`. Expected: FAIL (module not found).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `packages/core/src/chatMdInline.ts`. Emphasis is not here yet: `*` and `_` are ordinary characters until 176e, so `finish` is the whole of what turns the node list into the result.

```ts
import { destAt, markerAt, tickRuns } from "./chatMdScan";
import {
  MD_MAX_INLINE_DEPTH,
  type MdInline,
  type MdState,
  mdPlainText,
  safeLinkHref,
  take,
} from "./chatMdTypes";

// ── Markdown inlines: escapes, code spans, links, images, markers (U8c) ───────────────────

type Node = MdInline | Bracket;
interface Bracket {
  readonly type: "bracket";
  readonly image: boolean;
}
const hasLink = (nodes: readonly Node[]): boolean =>
  nodes.some(
    (n) => n.type === "link" || ((n.type === "em" || n.type === "strong") && hasLink(n.children))
  );

function finish(nodes: readonly Node[]): MdInline[] {
  const out: MdInline[] = [];
  const push = (n: MdInline): void => {
    const prev = out[out.length - 1];
    if (n.type === "text" && prev?.type === "text")
      out[out.length - 1] = { type: "text", text: prev.text + n.text };
    else if (!(n.type === "text" && n.text === "")) out.push(n);
  };
  for (const n of nodes) {
    if (n.type === "bracket") push({ type: "text", text: n.image ? "![" : "[" });
    else push(n);
  }
  return out;
}

/** Parse one run of inline text (a paragraph, a heading, a table cell) into at most the remaining node budget. */
export function parseInlines(source: string, st: MdState): MdInline[] {
  let src = source;
  if (st.streaming && st.markers) {
    const open = src.lastIndexOf("[[");
    if (open >= 0 && /^\[\[[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,128}\]?$/.test(src.slice(open))) {
      src = src.slice(0, open);
      if (src.endsWith(" ")) src = src.slice(0, -1);
    }
  }
  const out: Node[] = [];
  const ticks = tickRuns(src);
  let tick = 0;
  let buf = "";
  const flush = (): void => {
    if (buf !== "") out.push({ type: "text", text: buf });
    buf = "";
  };
  let i = 0;
  while (i < src.length) {
    const c = src[i] as string;
    if (c === "\\") {
      const nx = src[i + 1];
      if (nx === "\n") {
        flush();
        out.push({ type: "br" });
        i += 2;
      } else if (nx !== undefined && /[!-/:-@[-`{-~]/.test(nx)) {
        buf += nx;
        i += 2;
      } else {
        buf += c;
        i++;
      }
    } else if (c === "\n") {
      if (buf.endsWith("  ")) {
        buf = buf.trimEnd();
        flush();
        out.push({ type: "br" });
      } else buf = `${buf.trimEnd()}\n`;
      i++;
      while (src[i] === " ") i++;
    } else if (c === "`") {
      while (tick < ticks.length && (ticks[tick] as { pos: number }).pos < i) tick++;
      const run = ticks[tick] as { pos: number; len: number; next: number };
      const close = run.next < 0 ? undefined : (ticks[run.next] as { pos: number });
      if (!close) {
        buf += "`".repeat(run.len);
        i += run.len;
      } else {
        flush();
        let code = src.slice(i + run.len, close.pos).replace(/\n/g, " ");
        if (code.length > 2 && code.startsWith(" ") && code.endsWith(" ") && code.trim() !== "")
          code = code.slice(1, -1);
        out.push({ type: "code", text: code });
        i = close.pos + run.len;
      }
    } else if (c === "[" || (c === "!" && src[i + 1] === "[")) {
      const mk = st.markers && c === "[" ? markerAt(src, i) : undefined;
      if (mk) {
        flush();
        out.push({ type: "marker", id: mk.id });
        i = mk.end;
      } else {
        flush();
        const image = c === "!";
        out.push({ type: "bracket", image });
        i += image ? 2 : 1;
      }
    } else if (c === "]") {
      let bi = out.length - 1;
      while (bi >= 0 && (out[bi] as Node).type !== "bracket") bi--;
      const dest = bi >= 0 && src[i + 1] === "(" ? destAt(src, i + 1) : undefined;
      const pending = st.streaming && bi >= 0 && (i + 1 === src.length || dest === "eof");
      if (
        bi < 0 ||
        (dest === undefined && !pending) ||
        (dest !== undefined && dest !== "eof" ? false : !pending)
      ) {
        buf += "]";
        i++;
        continue;
      }
      flush();
      const bracket = out[bi] as Bracket;
      const inner = out.splice(bi);
      inner.shift();
      const kids = finish(inner);
      const label = kids.filter((n) => n.type !== "marker");
      if (dest === undefined || dest === "eof") {
        // streaming: `[label](half-written` or `[label]` at the very end: the label, nothing else, until it settles
        for (const n of label) out.push(n);
        i = dest === "eof" ? src.length : i + 1;
      } else if (bracket.image) {
        out.push({ type: "text", text: mdPlainText(label) });
        i = dest.end;
      } else if (hasLink(label)) {
        out.push({ type: "text", text: "[" }, ...label, { type: "text", text: "]" });
        i++;
      } else {
        const href = safeLinkHref(dest.href);
        if (href) out.push({ type: "link", href, children: label });
        else for (const n of label) out.push(n);
        i = dest.end;
      }
    } else {
      buf += c;
      i++;
    }
  }
  buf = buf.trimEnd();
  flush();
  return limit(finish(out), MD_MAX_INLINE_DEPTH, st);
}

/** Flatten nesting deeper than `depth` and charge every node to the budget (the rest is cut). */
function limit(nodes: readonly MdInline[], depth: number, st: MdState): MdInline[] {
  const out: MdInline[] = [];
  for (const n of nodes) {
    if ((n.type === "em" || n.type === "strong" || n.type === "link") && depth <= 1) {
      out.push(...limit(n.children, 1, st));
      continue;
    }
    if (!take(st)) break;
    out.push(
      n.type === "em" || n.type === "strong" || n.type === "link"
        ? { ...n, children: limit(n.children, depth - 1, st) }
        : n
    );
  }
  return out;
}
```

### Task 176e (loom-ui repo): markdown 3 of 9: emphasis and strong

**Repo and branch:** `~/workspace/loom-ui`, branch `feat/chat-md-emphasis`, cut from 176d, a draft PR, rebase-merged, stacked in the order of the task numbers (176b first). Same conventions as Task 176a: read loom-ui's `AGENTS.md` first (Loom is consumed built; `typecheck:tests` is a gate). The gate is `pnpm build && pnpm typecheck && pnpm typecheck:tests && pnpm test && pnpm lint:fix && pnpm lint` (build first: renderer packages resolve `@loom-ui/core` from its built `dist`). 

**Why (generic).** `*em*`, `**strong**`, `_em_` and `__strong__` with CommonMark's flanking and delimiter-stack rules (so `snake_case_word` and `2*3*4` behave), bounded: at most 200 delimiter runs are resolved per inline run (more stay literal), nesting is flattened past 4. It is its own PR because it is the one part of the inline parser with a non-trivial algorithm.

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/core/src/chatMdEmphasis.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/core/src/chatMdInline.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: 176d's `parseInlines`.
- Produces: `em` and `strong` nodes from `parseInlines`; unmatched delimiters stay literal characters; the link label and a reference marker may sit inside emphasis.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** `packages/core/src/chatMdEmphasis.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { parseInlines } from "./chatMdInline";
import type { MdInline } from "./chatMdTypes";

const inl = (t: string, o: { markers?: boolean; streaming?: boolean } = {}): readonly MdInline[] =>
  parseInlines(t, {
    nodes: 0,
    truncated: false,
    markers: o.markers === true,
    streaming: o.streaming === true,
  });
const txt = (text: string): MdInline => ({ type: "text", text });

describe("emphasis", () => {
  it("em and strong, with * and _", () => {
    expect(inl("a *b* **c** _e_ __f__")).toEqual([
      txt("a "),
      { type: "em", children: [txt("b")] },
      txt(" "),
      { type: "strong", children: [txt("c")] },
      txt(" "),
      { type: "em", children: [txt("e")] },
      txt(" "),
      { type: "strong", children: [txt("f")] },
    ]);
    expect(inl("***x***")).toEqual([
      { type: "em", children: [{ type: "strong", children: [txt("x")] }] },
    ]);
  });
  it("does not emphasise inside a word with _, or a lone *", () => {
    expect(inl("snake_case_word 2*3*4")).toEqual([
      txt("snake_case_word 2"),
      { type: "em", children: [txt("3")] },
      txt("4"),
    ]);
    expect(inl("a * b * c")).toEqual([txt("a * b * c")]);
  });
  it("leaves an unmatched delimiter as the literal characters", () => {
    expect(inl("**open and `tick")).toEqual([txt("**open and `tick")]);
    expect(inl("**a*")).toEqual([txt("*"), { type: "em", children: [txt("a")] }]);
    expect(inl("*a **b")).toEqual([txt("*a **b")]);
  });
  it("takes no emphasis inside a code span", () => {
    expect(inl("`**a**` **b**")).toEqual([
      { type: "code", text: "**a**" },
      txt(" "),
      { type: "strong", children: [txt("b")] },
    ]);
  });
  it("works inside a link label and around a reference marker", () => {
    expect(inl("[**m**](mailto:a@b.example)")).toEqual([
      {
        type: "link",
        href: "mailto:a@b.example",
        children: [{ type: "strong", children: [txt("m")] }],
      },
    ]);
    expect(inl("**x [[a_b]]**", { markers: true })).toEqual([
      { type: "strong", children: [txt("x "), { type: "marker", id: "a_b" }] },
    ]);
  });
  it("caps nesting", () => {
    const deep = inl(`${"**c ".repeat(10)}x${" c**".repeat(10)}`);
    const depth = (ns: readonly MdInline[]): number =>
      Math.max(0, ...ns.map((n) => ("children" in n ? 1 + depth(n.children) : 0)));
    expect(depth(deep)).toBeLessThanOrEqual(4);
  });
  it("is linear on adversarial delimiters, and caps the runs it resolves", () => {
    for (const c of [
      "*a ".repeat(30000),
      "_*".repeat(30000),
      "**".repeat(30000),
      "*a*".repeat(20000),
      "*a _b ".repeat(10000),
    ]) {
      const t = Date.now();
      inl(c);
      expect(Date.now() - t, c.slice(0, 6)).toBeLessThan(1500);
    }
    const literal = inl("*a*".repeat(300)).filter((n) => n.type === "em").length;
    expect(literal).toBeLessThanOrEqual(100);
  });
});
```

  ```ts
  /** Resolve emphasis over `nodes` (CommonMark's delimiter-stack rules) and turn what is left of delimiters and brackets into text. */
  function settle(nodes: Node[]): MdInline[] {
    let ci = 0;
    while (ci < nodes.length) {
      const closer = nodes[ci] as Node;
      if (closer.type !== "delim" || !closer.close || closer.count === 0) {
        ci++;
        continue;

      let opener: Delim | undefined;

          o.ch === closer.ch &&
          o.open &&
          o.count > 0 &&
          !(
            (o.close || closer.open) &&
            (o.count + closer.count) % 3 === 0 &&
            !(o.count % 3 === 0 && closer.count % 3 === 0)

          opener = o;
          break;

        oi--;
      }
      if (!opener) {
        ci++;
        continue;

      const use = opener.count >= 2 && closer.count >= 2 ? 2 : 1;
      const inner = finish(nodes.slice(oi + 1, ci));
      opener.count -= use;
      closer.count -= use;
      nodes.splice(oi + 1, ci - oi - 1, { type: use === 2 ? "strong" : "em", children: inner });
      ci = oi + 2;
      if (closer.count === 0) ci++;
    }
    return finish(nodes);
  }
  ```
